   130	    FileNotValid,
   131	    NotIncludeAllComponent(String),
   132	}
   133	
   134	/// Initializes the component system for a specific stage.
   135	///
   136	/// It collects all functions marked with the `init_component` macro, filters them
   137	/// according to the given stage, and invokes them in the correct order while honoring
   138	/// dependencies and priorities between crates.
   139	///
   140	/// The collection of ComponentInfo usually generate by `parse_metadata` macro.
   141	///
   142	/// ```rust
   143	///     component::init_all(component::InitStage::Bootstrap, component::parse_metadata!());
   144	/// ```
   145	///
   146	pub fn init_all(
   147	    stage: InitStage,
   148	    components: Vec<ComponentInfo>,
   149	) -> Result<(), ComponentSystemInitError> {
   150	    let components_info = parse_input(components);
   151	    match_and_call(stage, components_info)?;
   152	    Ok(())
   153	}
   154	
   155	fn parse_input(components: Vec<ComponentInfo>) -> BTreeMap<String, ComponentInfo> {
   156	    debug!("All component:{components:?}");
   157	    let mut out = BTreeMap::new();
   158	    for component in components {
   159	        out.insert(component.path.clone(), component);
   160	    }
   161	    out
   162	}
   163	
   164	/// Match the ComponentInfo with ComponentRegistry. The key is the relative path of one component
   165	fn match_and_call(
   166	    stage: InitStage,
   167	    mut components: BTreeMap<String, ComponentInfo>,
   168	) -> Result<(), ComponentSystemInitError> {
   169	    let mut infos = Vec::new();
   170	    for registry in inventory::iter::<ComponentRegistry> {
   171	        if registry.stage != stage {
   172	            continue;
   173	        }
   174	
   175	        // relative/path/to/comps/pci/src/lib.rs
   176	        let mut str: String = registry.path.to_owned();
   177	        str = str.replace('\\', "/");
   178	        // relative/path/to/comps/pci
   179	        // There are two cases, one in the test folder and one in the src folder.
   180	        // There may be multiple directories within the folder.

   219	    }

    32	/// Automatic generate all the component information
    33	pub fn component_generate() -> Vec<ComponentInfo> {
    34	    // extract components information
    35	    let mut metadata = metadata();
    36	
    37	    let mut component_packages = vec![];
    38	    let workspace_root = metadata["workspace_root"].as_str().unwrap();
    39	    let workspace_root = String::from_str(workspace_root).unwrap().replace('\\', "/");
    40	
    41	    let comps_name = get_components_name(&workspace_root, &metadata["packages"]);
    42	    for package in metadata["packages"].members_mut() {
    43	        let name = package["name"].as_str().unwrap();
    44	        if comps_name.contains(&name.to_string()) {
    45	            // remove useless depend
    46	            let mut depends = JsonValue::Array(Vec::new());
    47	            loop {
    48	                let depend = package["dependencies"].pop();
    49	                if depend == JsonValue::Null {
    50	                    break;
    51	                }
    52	                if comps_name.contains(&depend["name"].as_str().unwrap().to_string()) {
    53	                    depends.push(depend).unwrap();
    54	                }
    55	            }
    56	            package["dependencies"] = depends;
    57	            component_packages.push(package);
    58	        }
    59	    }
    60	
    61	    // calculate priority
    62	    let mut mapping: HashMap<String, u16> = HashMap::new();
    63	    let mut component_packages_map: HashMap<String, &mut JsonValue> = HashMap::new();
    64	    for i in component_packages.iter_mut() {
    65	        component_packages_map.insert(i["name"].as_str().unwrap().to_string(), i);
    66	    }
    67	
    68	    for (name, package) in component_packages_map.iter() {
    69	        if mapping.contains_key(package["name"].as_str().unwrap()) {
    70	            continue;
    71	        }
    72	        calculate_priority(&mut mapping, &component_packages_map, name.clone());
    73	    }
    74	    drop(component_packages_map);
    75	
    76	    // priority calculation complete
    77	    let mut components_info = Vec::new();
    78	    for package in component_packages {
    79	        let path = {
    80	            // Parse the package ID <https://doc.rust-lang.org/cargo/reference/pkgid-spec.html>
    81	            // and extract the path. Let's take `path+file:///path/to/comps/pci#aster-pci@0.1.0`
    82	            // as an example package ID.
    83	            let id = package["id"].as_str().unwrap();
    84	            // Remove the prefix `path+file://`.
    85	            assert!(id.starts_with("path+file://"));
    86	            let id = id.trim_start_matches("path+file://");
    87	            // Remove the fragment part `#aster-pci@0.1.0`. Note that the package name part
    88	            // may be missing if the directory name is the same as the package name.
    89	            id.split(['#', '@']).next().unwrap()
    90	        };
    91	        let component_info = {
    92	            let package_name = package["name"].as_str().unwrap().to_string();
    93	            ComponentInfo {
    94	                name: package_name.clone(),
    95	                path: PathBuf::from(&workspace_root)
    96	                    .join(path)
    97	                    .to_str()
    98	                    .unwrap()
    99	                    .to_string(),
   100	                priority: *mapping.get(&package_name).unwrap(),
   101	            }
   102	        };
   103	        components_info.push(component_info)
   104	    }
   105	
   106	    components_info
   107	}
   108	
   109	/// Get the path to the Components.toml file
   110	pub fn get_component_toml_path() -> TokenStream {
   111	    let metadata = metadata();
   112	    let workspace_root = metadata["workspace_root"].as_str().unwrap();
   113	    let mut workspace_root = String::from_str(workspace_root)
   114	        .unwrap()
   115	        .replace('\\', "/")
   116	        .add("/")
   117	        .add(COMPONENT_FILE_NAME)
   118	        .add("\"");
   119	    workspace_root.insert(0, '\"');
   120	    TokenStream::from_str(workspace_root.as_str()).unwrap()
   121	}
   122	
   123	fn is_component(package: &JsonValue) -> bool {
   124	    for depend in package["dependencies"].members() {
   125	        if depend["name"].as_str().unwrap() == "component" {
   126	            return true;
   127	        }
   128	    }
   129	    false
   130	}
   131	
   132	/// Get all the components name, this function will also check if the Components.toml contain all the components.
   133	fn get_components_name(workspace_root: &str, packages: &JsonValue) -> Vec<String> {
   134	    let file_components_name = read_component_file(workspace_root);
   135	    let mut comps_name = Vec::new();
   136	    for package in packages.members() {
   137	        if is_component(package) {
   138	            if !file_components_name.contains(&package["name"].as_str().unwrap().to_string()) {
   139	                // if the package is in the workspace_root
   140	                if package["id"].as_str().unwrap().contains(workspace_root) {
   141	                    panic!(
   142	                        "Package {} in the workspace that not written in the {} file",
   143	                        package["name"].as_str().unwrap(),
   144	                        COMPONENT_FILE_NAME
   145	                    );
   146	                }
   147	            }
   148	            comps_name.push(package["name"].as_str().unwrap().to_string());
   149	        }
   150	    }
   151	    comps_name
   152	}
   153	
   154	/// read component file, return all the components name
   155	fn read_component_file(workspace_root: &str) -> Vec<String> {
   156	    let component_toml: toml::Value = {
   157	        let mut component_file_path = workspace_root.to_owned();
   158	        component_file_path.push('/');
   159	        component_file_path.push_str(COMPONENT_FILE_NAME);
   160	        let mut file = File::open(component_file_path)
   161	            .expect("Components.toml file not found, please check if the file exists");
   162	        let mut str_val = String::new();
   163	        file.read_to_string(&mut str_val).unwrap();
   164	        toml::from_str(&str_val).unwrap()
   165	    };
   166	    for (name, value) in component_toml.as_table().unwrap() {
   167	        if name.as_str() == "components" {
   168	            return value
   169	                .as_table()
   170	                .unwrap()
   171	                .values()
   172	                .map(|value| {
   173	                    value
   174	                        .as_table()
   175	                        .unwrap()
   176	                        .values()
   177	                        .map(|str_val| str_val.as_str().unwrap().to_string())
   178	                        .collect()
   179	                })
   180	                .collect();
   181	        }
   182	    }
   183	    panic!("Components.toml file not valid")
   184	}
   185	
   186	/// calculate the priority of one node
   187	fn calculate_priority(
   188	    prioritys: &mut HashMap<String, u16>,
   189	    package_mapping: &HashMap<String, &mut JsonValue>,
   190	    node_name: String,
   191	) -> u16 {
   192	    if prioritys.contains_key(&node_name) {
   193	        return *prioritys.get(&node_name).unwrap();
   194	    }
   195	
   196	    let package = &package_mapping[&node_name];
   197	    let mut lowest_priority: u16 = 0;
   198	    for depends in package["dependencies"].members() {
   199	        lowest_priority = lowest_priority.max(
   200	            calculate_priority(
   201	                prioritys,
   202	                package_mapping,
   203	                depends["name"].as_str().unwrap().to_string(),
   204	            ) + 1,
   205	        );
   206	    }
   207	
   208	    prioritys.insert(node_name.to_string(), lowest_priority);
   209	    lowest_priority
   210	}

   217	
   218	    if !output.status.success() {
   219	        panic!("cannot get metadata");
   220	    }

     1	// SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0
     2	
     3	//！This crate defines the component system related macros.
     4	
     5	#![feature(proc_macro_diagnostic)]
     6	#![deny(unsafe_code)]
     7	
     8	mod init_comp;
     9	mod priority;
    10	
    11	use init_comp::ComponentInitFunction;
    12	use proc_macro::TokenStream;
    13	use quote::quote;
    14	use syn::parse_macro_input;
    15	
    16	pub(crate) const COMPONENT_FILE_NAME: &str = "Components.toml";
    17	
    18	/// Register a function to be called when the component system is initialized. The function should not public.
    19	///
    20	/// You can specify the initialization stage by:
    21	/// - `#[init_component]` or `#[init_component(bootstrap)]` - the **Bootstrap** stage
    22	/// - `#[init_component(kthread)]` - the **Kthread** stage
    23	/// - `#[init_component(process)]` - the **Process** stage
    24	///
    25	/// Example:
    26	/// ```rust
    27	/// #[init_component]
    28	/// fn init() -> Result<(), component::ComponentInitError> {
    29	///     Ok(())
    30	/// }
    31	/// ```
    32	///
    33	/// It will expand to
    34	/// ```rust
    35	/// fn init() -> Result<(), component::ComponentInitError> {
    36	///     Ok(())
    37	/// }
    38	///
    39	/// component::submit!(component::ComponentRegistry::new(component::InitStage::Bootstrap, &init, file!()));
    40	/// ```
    41	/// The priority will calculate automatically
    42	///
    43	#[proc_macro_attribute]
    44	pub fn init_component(args: TokenStream, input: TokenStream) -> proc_macro::TokenStream {
    45	    let stage = match args.to_string().as_str() {
    46	        "" | "bootstrap" => quote! { Bootstrap },
    47	        "kthread" => quote! { Kthread },
    48	        "process" => quote! { Process },
    49	        _ => panic!("Invalid argument for init_component"),
    50	    };
    51	    let function = parse_macro_input!(input as ComponentInitFunction);
    52	    let function_name = &function.function_name;
    53	    quote! {
    54	        #function
    55	
    56	        component::submit!(component::ComponentRegistry::new(
    57	            component::InitStage::#stage,
    58	            &#function_name,
    59	            file!())
    60	        );
    61	    }
    62	    .into()
    63	}
    64	
    65	/// Automatically generate all component information required by the component system.
    66	///
    67	/// It mainly uses the output of the command `cargo metadata` to automatically generate information about all components, and also checks whether `Components.toml` contains all the components.
    68	///
    69	/// It is often used with `component::init_all`.
    70	///
    71	/// Example:
    72	///
    73	/// ```rust
    74	///     component::init_all(component::InitStage::Bootstrap, component::parse_metadata!());
    75	/// ```
    76	///
    77	#[proc_macro]
    78	pub fn parse_metadata(_: TokenStream) -> proc_macro::TokenStream {
    79	    let out = priority::component_generate();
    80	    let path = priority::get_component_toml_path();
    81	    quote! {
    82	        {
    83	            include_str!(#path);
    84	            extern crate alloc;
    85	            alloc::vec![
    86	                #(component::ComponentInfo::new #out),*
    87	            ]
    88	        }
    89	    }
    90	    .into()
    91	}

# Summary

<!-- SUMMARY -->

## Maintainability

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/Cargo.toml` line 13

> ```diff
> +inventory = { git = "https://github.com/asterinas/inventory", rev = "9dce587" }
> ```

`workspace-deps` (minor): `inventory` is declared directly in `kernel/comps/cmdline/Cargo.toml`, even though the same Git dependency is already used by another workspace crate. That duplicates the revision policy and makes future updates easy to miss.

**Fix.** Move `inventory = { git = "https://github.com/asterinas/inventory", rev = "9dce587" }` to the root `[workspace.dependencies]`, then use `inventory.workspace = true` here and in the existing `component` crate.

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/lib.rs` line 145

> ```diff
> /// Helper for defining kernel command-line parameter.
> #[macro_export]
> macro_rules! __define_param {
> ```

`information-hiding` (minor): `__define_param!` is an implementation helper, but `#[macro_export]` publishes it as part of the crate API. Callers can bypass the stable `define_*_param!` macros and become coupled to `setup_*` internals.

**Fix.** Keep only the stable `define_*_param!` macros as the intended public interface. At minimum, mark `__define_param!` with `#[doc(hidden)]`; preferably avoid exporting the helper by moving the shared registration logic behind a private constructor/helper that the public macros call.

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/lib.rs` line 179

> ```diff
> pub fn argv(&self) -> &Vec<CString> {
>     &self.argv
> }
> 
> /// Gets the environment vector (`envp`) of the init process.
> pub fn envp(&self) -> &Vec<CString> {
> ```

`information-hiding` (minor): `argv()` and `envp()` return `&Vec<CString>`, exposing the storage type even though callers only need read-only sequence access. This makes a future storage change a public API break.

**Fix.** Return slices from both getters: `pub fn argv(&self) -> &[CString]` and `pub fn envp(&self) -> &[CString]`.

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/lib.rs` line 229

> ```diff
> /// Parse a single-value key-value parameter (e.g., log_level=3).
> pub trait ParseParamValue: Sized {
> ...
> /// Parse a repeatable key-value parameter (e.g., "console=ttyS0 console=ttyS1").
> pub trait ParseRepeatableParamValue: Sized {
> ...
> /// Parse a flag parameter (e.g., "ro", "debug", "nokaslr").
> pub trait ParseFlag: Sized {
> ```

`rfc1574-summary` (nit): The public trait docs for `ParseParamValue`, `ParseRepeatableParamValue`, and `ParseFlag` start with imperative-style `Parse ...` summaries. For traits, the summary should be a noun phrase, so rustdoc reads like an API description rather than an instruction.

**Fix.** Rewrite the summaries as noun phrases, for example `A parser for single-value key-value parameters.` and `A parser for repeatable key-value parameters.`.

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/lib.rs` line 286

> ```diff
> pub struct KernelParam {
>     pub name: &'static str,
>     pub setup: fn(occurrences: &[(&str, Option<&str>)]),
>     pub early: bool,
> }
> ```

`rust-type-invariants` (minor): `KernelParam` exposes boot ordering as a bare `bool` field, so every registration must remember that `early: true` means early and `early: false` means late. That policy is clearer and harder to misuse as a typed phase.

**Fix.** Replace `early: bool` with an enum such as `ParamPhase::{Early, Late}`, and have the registration macros set `phase: ParamPhase::Early` or `phase: ParamPhase::Late`.

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/types.rs` line 8

> ```diff
> #![allow(dead_code)]
> ```

`narrow-lint-suppression` (minor): `#![allow(dead_code)]` suppresses dead-code diagnostics for the entire `types` module, including future additions. The rest of the tree uses `#[expect(dead_code)]`, and broad `allow` hides exactly which items are intentionally unused.

**Fix.** Remove the module-wide `allow`; put `#[expect(dead_code)]` on only the currently unused items, with a short reason or concrete planned use where appropriate.

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/types.rs` line 24

> ```diff
> #[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
> pub struct CpuList {
> ```

`alphabetical-attrs` (nit): The new `#[derive(...)]` lists are not sorted alphabetically; this one starts with `Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq`, and the same ordering appears again on nearby public types.

**Fix.** Sort the derive traits alphabetically, for example `#[derive(Clone, Debug, Eq, PartialEq)]`; apply the same ordering to `CpuListSegment`, `MetricU64`, and the new derives in `lib.rs`.

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/types.rs` line 31

> ```diff
> pub struct CpuListSegment {
>     pub start: u32,
>     pub end: u32,
>     /// Step within range, default 1.
>     pub stride: u32,
>     /// Optional group size (the `/N` part). When present, stride selection is
>     /// applied within each group window.
>     pub group: Option<u32>,
> }
> ```

`rust-type-invariants` (major): `CpuListSegment` exposes all fields publicly, so any caller can construct invalid states such as `stride: 0`, `group: Some(0)`, or `start > end`. That leaks representation details and forces methods like `contains()` and `expand_bounded()` to defend against values the parser would never create.

**Fix.** Make the fields private and expose getters such as `start()`, `end()`, `stride()`, and `group()`. Construct `CpuListSegment` only through the parser or a validating constructor.

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/types.rs` line 270

> ```diff
> Some('K') => 1024u64,
> Some('M') => 1024u64.pow(2),
> Some('G') => 1024u64.pow(3),
> Some('T') => 1024u64.pow(4),
> Some('P') => 1024u64.pow(5),
> ```

`no-magic-number` (minor): `1024` encodes the binary metric base for `MetricU64` and is repeated across the parser and tests. The unit policy should have one semantic representation instead of several bare literals.

**Fix.** Introduce named constants such as `const KIB: u64 = 1024;`, `const MIB: u64 = KIB.pow(2);`, and use those constants in the suffix table and expected test values.

## Correctness

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/lib.rs` line 412

> ```diff
> let mut grouped: BTreeMap<String, Vec<(&str, Option<&str>)>> = BTreeMap::new();
> ...
> for (name, occurrences) in &grouped {
>     if let Some(param) = registry.get(name.as_str()) {
>         recognized.push((*param, occurrences));
>     } else {
>         ...
>     } else if let Some((key, Some(value))) = occurrences.last().copied() {
>         let envp_entry = CString::new(key.to_string() + "=" + value).unwrap();
>         result.envp.push(envp_entry);
>     } else if let Some((key, None)) = occurrences.last().copied() {
>         let argv_entry = CString::new(key.to_string()).unwrap();
>         result.argv.push(argv_entry);
>     }
> ```

Incorrect argument forwarding (major): `dispatch_params()` iterates unknown parameters through the `grouped` `BTreeMap` and forwards only `occurrences.last()`, so init arguments before `--` are no longer passed in command-line order and duplicates are collapsed. A concrete boot line like `b a -- /init` produces `argv` entries ordered as `a`, `b`, and `foo foo -- /init` forwards only one `foo`, even though Linux-style unknown bare parameters are passed to `init` as arguments as they appear.

**Fix.** Keep a separate ordered list of parsed tokens for unknown-parameter forwarding, or forward unrecognized entries during the original token scan while still grouping only recognized parameters for handler dispatch. Add a boot test that checks `argv` order and duplicate unknown arguments, not just membership.

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/types.rs` line 167

> ```diff
> let in_group_offset = offset % g;
> in_group_offset.is_multiple_of(s.stride)
> ```

Incorrect parsing (major): `CpuList` treats the `:U/G` suffix as a stride inside each group, but Linux CPU-list syntax uses `U` as the number of used CPUs per `G`-sized group. For `100-2000:2/25`, this code makes `contains(101)` false and `contains(102)` true, while the Linux-style expansion should start `100,101,125,126,...`.

**Fix.** Represent the suffix as `used` and `group`, require `used <= group`, implement containment as `(cpu - start) % group < used`, and update `expand_bounded()` to emit the first `used` CPUs from each `group` window. Replace the current grouped tests with a case such as `100-2000:2/25` that checks `100`, `101`, `125`, and `126` are included while `102` is not.

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/types.rs` line 275

> ```diff
> Some('T') => 1024u64.pow(4),
> Some('P') => 1024u64.pow(5),
> _ => return Err(ParamError::InvalidValue),
> ```

Incorrect parsing (minor): `MetricU64` rejects the Linux metric suffix `E` even though `1E` is a valid `u64` value (`1024^6`). Any parameter using this value type will reject a valid command-line size such as `mem=1E` or another `MetricU64`-backed setting.

**Fix.** Add `Some('E') => 1024u64.pow(6)` and tests for both `1E` success and an overflowing `E` value returning `ParamError::InvalidValue` through `checked_mul()`.

## Security

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/lib.rs` line 152

> ```diff
> ```diff
> +pub use inventory::submit;
> ...
> +            $crate::submit! {
> +                $crate::KernelParam { name: $name, setup: __kparam_setup, early: false }
> +            }
> ```
> ```

`deny-unsafe-kernel` (critical): `__define_param!` routes every kernel-parameter registration through `inventory::submit!`. That macro expands in the calling kernel crate to an `unsafe { ErasedNode::submit(...) }` constructor and relies on `inventory`'s raw global linked-list invariants, so the new parameter framework introduces hidden unsafe code into `kernel/` instead of confining it to `ostd/`.

**Fix.** Remove `inventory` from the kernel-side API. Use an explicit safe registry/list for the current parameters, or move the unsafe linker-section inventory machinery behind a small safe API in `ostd/` and have `kernel/` call only that safe wrapper.

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/lib.rs` line 481

> ```diff
> ```diff
> +define_unimplemented_param!(
> +    "tsc",
> +    "no_timer_check",
> +    "reboot",
> +    "pci",
> +    "debug",
> +    "panic",
> +    "nr_cpus",
> +    "selinux",
> +    "initrd"
> +);
> ```
> ```

`validate-at-boundaries` (major): `selinux` is registered as a recognized kernel parameter but `setup_unimplemented()` accepts every occurrence by only logging a warning. A boot line such as `selinux=1` requests enabling a Linux security module, yet Asterinas continues booting without SELinux enforcement; invalid values such as `selinux=garbage` are accepted the same way.

`validate-at-boundaries` (major): `selinux` is registered as a recognized kernel parameter but `setup_unimplemented()` accepts every occurrence by only logging a warning. A boot line such as `selinux=1` requests enabling a Linux security module, yet Asterinas continues booting without SELinux enforcement; invalid values such as `selinux=garbage` are accepted the same way.

## Documentation

### `book/src/kernel/linux-compatibility/kernel-parameters.md` line 24

> ```diff
> 22 Select console devices for kernel messages.
> 23 This parameter may be specified multiple times.
> 24 Kernel messages are delivered to each listed console.
> ...
> 105 // TODO: Support specifying multiple TTY devices, e.g., "console=hvc0 console=tty0".
> 106 let console_name = CONSOLES
> 107     .get()
> 108     .and_then(|consoles| consoles.first().map(|s| s.as_str()))
> ```

`linux-compat-docs` (major): The `console` entry promises that repeated `console=` values receive kernel messages, but the implementation only reads `CONSOLES.first()` for `/dev/console`, and the logger sends to all registered consoles independently of `console=`. A boot line such as `console=ttyS0 console=hvc0` is documented as selecting both consoles, but only `ttyS0` is used by `SystemConsole`.

**Fix.** Update `book/src/kernel/linux-compatibility/kernel-parameters.md` to describe the current behavior: `console=` is repeatable syntactically, but Asterinas currently uses only the first value for `/dev/console` and defaults/falls back to `tty0`. Remove the claim that kernel messages are delivered to every listed console unless the code is changed to implement that behavior.

### `book/src/kernel/linux-compatibility/kernel-parameters.md` line 62

> ```diff
> 60 Valid values:
> 61 - `1`, `on`, `yes`, `true` or no value — treat the i8042 controller as present (force probing)
> 62 - `0`, `off`, `no`, `false` - treat the i8042 controller as absent (skip probing)
> ...
> 132 if !Self::is_present_acpi() {
> 144     if !Self::is_present_cmdline() {
> 149         return Err(I8042ControllerError::NotPresent);
> 156 } else {
> 157     log::info!("ACPI says i8042 controller is present");
> ```

`linux-compat-docs` (major): The `i8042.exist` docs claim that `i8042.exist=0` forces the controller absent, but `I8042_EXIST` is only consulted inside the `!is_present_acpi()` branch. If ACPI reports the controller as present, `i8042.exist=0` is ignored and probing still proceeds, so the documented skip-probing mode does not exist.

**Fix.** Either implement the documented force-absent behavior, or change the parameter documentation to say that `i8042.exist` only forces probing when ACPI reports the controller absent, and that false values merely do not force probing.

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/lib.rs` line 473

> ```diff
> 470 // Placeholders for recognized but unimplemented kernel command-line parameters; these
> 471 // consume matching parameters so they are not forwarded to init and avoid unexpected
> 472 // boot behavior. A warning is emitted when such parameters appear.
> 473 define_unimplemented_param!(
> 474     "tsc",
> 475     "no_timer_check",
> 476     "reboot",
> 477     "pci",
> 478     "debug",
> 479     "panic",
> 480     "nr_cpus",
> 481     "selinux",
> 482     "initrd"
> ```

`linux-compat-docs` (major): This change registers `tsc`, `no_timer_check`, `reboot`, `pci`, `debug`, `panic`, `nr_cpus`, `selinux`, and `initrd` as recognized kernel parameters that are consumed and warned about, but `book/src/kernel/linux-compatibility/kernel-parameters.md` omits them. Users cannot tell from the compatibility docs that these Linux parameter names will no longer be forwarded to `init`.

**Fix.** Add a documented section for recognized-but-unimplemented kernel parameters, listing these names and saying that Asterinas consumes them and emits a warning without implementing Linux behavior; or stop registering them in this change until the compatibility page is updated.

# Summary

This series makes the kernel command line extensible through registered parameters, adds reusable parsers, wires `init`, `console`, and `i8042.exist` into that framework, improves early logging fallback, and documents the initial kernel-parameter surface.

The highest-priority issue is the new `inventory::submit!` path in `kernel/`: it expands unsafe linked-list registration machinery in safe kernel crates, which violates the architecture boundary that keeps unsafe code out of `kernel/`. The next correctness risks are in command-line semantics: unknown arguments are grouped through a `BTreeMap`, which changes order and drops duplicates before forwarding to `init`, and the Linux-style `CpuList` parser implements `:U/G` as a stride rather than "used CPUs per group." The documentation also overstates current behavior for `console=` and `i8042.exist`, and omits the newly consumed but unimplemented Linux parameter names.

Structurally, the new command-line API is moving in a useful direction, but it exposes several implementation details: a direct non-workspace dependency, an exported helper macro, `&Vec` getters, bare boolean phase state, public `CpuListSegment` fields, broad lint suppression, and repeated metric constants. Tightening those interfaces would make the parser easier to evolve once more parameters are added.

## Maintainability

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/Cargo.toml` line 13

> ```diff
> +inventory = { git = "https://github.com/asterinas/inventory", rev = "9dce587" }
> ```

`workspace-deps` (minor): `inventory` is declared directly in `kernel/comps/cmdline/Cargo.toml`, even though the same Git dependency is already used by another workspace crate. That duplicates the revision policy and makes future updates easy to miss.

**Fix.** Move `inventory = { git = "https://github.com/asterinas/inventory", rev = "9dce587" }` to the root `[workspace.dependencies]`, then use `inventory.workspace = true` here and in the existing `component` crate.

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/lib.rs` line 145

> ```diff
> /// Helper for defining kernel command-line parameter.
> #[macro_export]
> macro_rules! __define_param {
> ```

`information-hiding` (minor): `__define_param!` is an implementation helper, but `#[macro_export]` publishes it as part of the crate API. Callers can bypass the stable `define_*_param!` macros and become coupled to `setup_*` internals.

Incorrect parsing (minor): `MetricU64` rejects the Linux metric suffix `E` even though `1E` is a valid `u64` value (`1024^6`). Any parameter using this value type will reject a valid command-line size such as `mem=1E` or another `MetricU64`-backed setting.

**Fix.** Add `Some('E') => 1024u64.pow(6)` and tests for both `1E` success and an overflowing `E` value returning `ParamError::InvalidValue` through `checked_mul()`.

## Security

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/lib.rs` line 152

> ```diff
> +pub use inventory::submit;
> ...
> +            $crate::submit! {
> +                $crate::KernelParam { name: $name, setup: __kparam_setup, early: false }
> +            }
> ```

`deny-unsafe-kernel` (critical): `__define_param!` routes every kernel-parameter registration through `inventory::submit!`. That macro expands in the calling kernel crate to an `unsafe { ErasedNode::submit(...) }` constructor and relies on `inventory`'s raw global linked-list invariants, so the new parameter framework introduces hidden unsafe code into `kernel/` instead of confining it to `ostd/`.

**Fix.** Remove `inventory` from the kernel-side API. Use an explicit safe registry/list for the current parameters, or move the unsafe linker-section inventory machinery behind a small safe API in `ostd/` and have `kernel/` call only that safe wrapper.

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/lib.rs` line 481

> ```diff
> +define_unimplemented_param!(
> +    "tsc",
> +    "no_timer_check",
> +    "reboot",
> +    "pci",
> +    "debug",
> +    "panic",
> +    "nr_cpus",
> +    "selinux",
> +    "initrd"
> +);
> ```

`validate-at-boundaries` (major): `selinux` is registered as a recognized kernel parameter but `setup_unimplemented()` accepts every occurrence by only logging a warning. A boot line such as `selinux=1` requests enabling a Linux security module, yet Asterinas continues booting without SELinux enforcement; invalid values such as `selinux=garbage` are accepted the same way.

**Fix.** Do not consume security-enabling parameters as generic unimplemented placeholders. Either remove `selinux` from this list so it is not presented as recognized, or add a dedicated parser that validates `0`/`1` and fails closed for `selinux=1` until SELinux is implemented.

# Expected defects

1. location: kernel/comps/cmdline/Cargo.toml lines 13 (persona: maintainability, grounding: workspace-deps, severity: minor)
   defect: `kernel/comps/cmdline/Cargo.toml` declares `inventory` directly with a Git URL and revision. Shared dependencies should be declared once in the workspace `[workspace.dependencies]` table and referenced from member crates with `.workspace = true`; this is especially important here because the same `inventory` crate is already used elsewhere with the same source.
   MATCH IF: A reviewer should flag the direct `inventory` dependency in the member crate and ask for a workspace dependency referenced with `inventory.workspace = true`.

2. location: kernel/comps/cmdline/src/types.rs lines 8 (persona: maintainability, grounding: narrow-lint-suppression, severity: minor)

That hides unused-code warnings not only for the currently intentional parser helpers, but also for future accidental additions to the module, making the lint suppression broader than the code that needs it.
   MATCH IF: A reviewer should flag the module-wide `#![allow(dead_code)]` and ask for the `dead_code` suppression to be narrowed to the specific item that needs it.

3. location: kernel/comps/cmdline/src/types.rs lines 31 (persona: maintainability, grounding: getter-encapsulation, severity: minor)
   defect: `CpuListSegment` exposes `start`, `end`, `stride`, and `group` as public fields even though the parser enforces invariants such as `start <= end`, nonzero `stride`, and nonzero `group`. Public fields let external code construct invalid segments and make the representation part of the API.
   MATCH IF: A reviewer should flag the public fields on `CpuListSegment` and ask for private fields with getters or another checked interface that preserves the parser invariants.

# Summary

This series makes the kernel command line extensible through registered parameters, adds reusable parsers, wires `init`, `console`, and `i8042.exist` into that framework, improves early logging fallback, and documents the initial kernel-parameter surface.

The highest-priority issue is the new `inventory::submit!` path in `kernel/`: it expands unsafe linked-list registration machinery in safe kernel crates, which violates the architecture boundary that keeps unsafe code out of `kernel/`. The next correctness risks are in command-line semantics: unknown arguments are grouped through a `BTreeMap`, which changes order and drops duplicates before forwarding to `init`, and the Linux-style `CpuList` parser implements `:U/G` as a stride rather than "used CPUs per group." The documentation also overstates current behavior for `console=` and `i8042.exist`, and omits the newly consumed but unimplemented Linux parameter names.

Structurally, the new command-line API is moving in a useful direction, but it exposes several implementation details: a direct non-workspace dependency, an exported helper macro, `&Vec` getters, bare boolean phase state, public `CpuListSegment` fields, broad lint suppression, and repeated metric constants. Tightening those interfaces would make the parser easier to evolve once more parameters are added.

## Maintainability

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/Cargo.toml` line 13

> ```diff
> +inventory = { git = "https://github.com/asterinas/inventory", rev = "9dce587" }
> ```

`workspace-deps` (minor): `inventory` is declared directly in `kernel/comps/cmdline/Cargo.toml`, even though the same Git dependency is already used by another workspace crate. That duplicates the revision policy and makes future updates easy to miss.

**Fix.** Move `inventory = { git = "https://github.com/asterinas/inventory", rev = "9dce587" }` to the root `[workspace.dependencies]`, then use `inventory.workspace = true` here and in the existing `component` crate.

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/lib.rs` line 145

> ```diff
> /// Helper for defining kernel command-line parameter.
> #[macro_export]
> macro_rules! __define_param {
> ```

`information-hiding` (minor): `__define_param!` is an implementation helper, but `#[macro_export]` publishes it as part of the crate API. Callers can bypass the stable `define_*_param!` macros and become coupled to `setup_*` internals.

**Fix.** Keep only the stable `define_*_param!` macros as the intended public interface. At minimum, mark `__define_param!` with `#[doc(hidden)]`; preferably avoid exporting the helper by moving the shared registration logic behind a private constructor/helper that the public macros call.

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/lib.rs` line 179

> ```diff
> pub fn argv(&self) -> &Vec<CString> {
>     &self.argv
> }
> 
> /// Gets the environment vector (`envp`) of the init process.
> pub fn envp(&self) -> &Vec<CString> {
> ```

`information-hiding` (minor): `argv()` and `envp()` return `&Vec<CString>`, exposing the storage type even though callers only need read-only sequence access. This makes a future storage change a public API break.

**Fix.** Return slices from both getters: `pub fn argv(&self) -> &[CString]` and `pub fn envp(&self) -> &[CString]`.

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/lib.rs` line 229

> ```diff
> /// Parse a single-value key-value parameter (e.g., log_level=3).
> pub trait ParseParamValue: Sized {
> ...
> /// Parse a repeatable key-value parameter (e.g., "console=ttyS0 console=ttyS1").
> pub trait ParseRepeatableParamValue: Sized {
> ...
> /// Parse a flag parameter (e.g., "ro", "debug", "nokaslr").
> pub trait ParseFlag: Sized {
> ```

`rfc1574-summary` (nit): The public trait docs for `ParseParamValue`, `ParseRepeatableParamValue`, and `ParseFlag` start with imperative-style `Parse ...` summaries. For traits, the summary should be a noun phrase, so rustdoc reads like an API description rather than an instruction.

**Fix.** Rewrite the summaries as noun phrases, for example `A parser for single-value key-value parameters.` and `A parser for repeatable key-value parameters.`.

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/lib.rs` line 286

> ```diff
> pub struct KernelParam {
>     pub name: &'static str,
>     pub setup: fn(occurrences: &[(&str, Option<&str>)]),
>     pub early: bool,
> }
> ```

`rust-type-invariants` (minor): `KernelParam` exposes boot ordering as a bare `bool` field, so every registration must remember that `early: true` means early and `early: false` means late. That policy is clearer and harder to misuse as a typed phase.

**Fix.** Replace `early: bool` with an enum such as `ParamPhase::{Early, Late}`, and have the registration macros set `phase: ParamPhase::Early` or `phase: ParamPhase::Late`.

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/types.rs` line 8

> ```diff
> #![allow(dead_code)]
> ```

`narrow-lint-suppression` (minor): `#![allow(dead_code)]` suppresses dead-code diagnostics for the entire `types` module, including future additions. The rest of the tree uses `#[expect(dead_code)]`, and broad `allow` hides exactly which items are intentionally unused.

**Fix.** Remove the module-wide `allow`; put `#[expect(dead_code)]` on only the currently unused items, with a short reason or concrete planned use where appropriate.

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/types.rs` line 24

> ```diff
> #[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
> pub struct CpuList {
> ```

`alphabetical-attrs` (nit): The new `#[derive(...)]` lists are not sorted alphabetically; this one starts with `Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq`, and the same ordering appears again on nearby public types.

**Fix.** Sort the derive traits alphabetically, for example `#[derive(Clone, Debug, Eq, PartialEq)]`; apply the same ordering to `CpuListSegment`, `MetricU64`, and the new derives in `lib.rs`.

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/types.rs` line 31

> ```diff
> pub struct CpuListSegment {
>     pub start: u32,
>     pub end: u32,
>     /// Step within range, default 1.
>     pub stride: u32,
>     /// Optional group size (the `/N` part). When present, stride selection is
>     /// applied within each group window.
>     pub group: Option<u32>,
> }
> ```

`rust-type-invariants` (major): `CpuListSegment` exposes all fields publicly, so any caller can construct invalid states such as `stride: 0`, `group: Some(0)`, or `start > end`. That leaks representation details and forces methods like `contains()` and `expand_bounded()` to defend against values the parser would never create.

**Fix.** Make the fields private and expose getters such as `start()`, `end()`, `stride()`, and `group()`. Construct `CpuListSegment` only through the parser or a validating constructor.

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/types.rs` line 270

> ```diff
> Some('K') => 1024u64,
> Some('M') => 1024u64.pow(2),
> Some('G') => 1024u64.pow(3),
> Some('T') => 1024u64.pow(4),
> Some('P') => 1024u64.pow(5),
> ```

`no-magic-number` (minor): `1024` encodes the binary metric base for `MetricU64` and is repeated across the parser and tests. The unit policy should have one semantic representation instead of several bare literals.

**Fix.** Introduce named constants such as `const KIB: u64 = 1024;`, `const MIB: u64 = KIB.pow(2);`, and use those constants in the suffix table and expected test values.

## Correctness

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/lib.rs` line 412

> ```diff
> let mut grouped: BTreeMap<String, Vec<(&str, Option<&str>)>> = BTreeMap::new();
> ...
> for (name, occurrences) in &grouped {
>     if let Some(param) = registry.get(name.as_str()) {
>         recognized.push((*param, occurrences));
>     } else {
>         ...
>     } else if let Some((key, Some(value))) = occurrences.last().copied() {
>         let envp_entry = CString::new(key.to_string() + "=" + value).unwrap();
>         result.envp.push(envp_entry);
>     } else if let Some((key, None)) = occurrences.last().copied() {
>         let argv_entry = CString::new(key.to_string()).unwrap();
>         result.argv.push(argv_entry);
>     }
> ```

Incorrect argument forwarding (major): `dispatch_params()` iterates unknown parameters through the `grouped` `BTreeMap` and forwards only `occurrences.last()`, so init arguments before `--` are no longer passed in command-line order and duplicates are collapsed. A concrete boot line like `b a -- /init` produces `argv` entries ordered as `a`, `b`, and `foo foo -- /init` forwards only one `foo`, even though Linux-style unknown bare parameters are passed to `init` as arguments as they appear.

**Fix.** Keep a separate ordered list of parsed tokens for unknown-parameter forwarding, or forward unrecognized entries during the original token scan while still grouping only recognized parameters for handler dispatch. Add a boot test that checks `argv` order and duplicate unknown arguments, not just membership.

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/types.rs` line 167

> ```diff
> let in_group_offset = offset % g;
> in_group_offset.is_multiple_of(s.stride)
> ```

Incorrect parsing (major): `CpuList` treats the `:U/G` suffix as a stride inside each group, but Linux CPU-list syntax uses `U` as the number of used CPUs per `G`-sized group. For `100-2000:2/25`, this code makes `contains(101)` false and `contains(102)` true, while the Linux-style expansion should start `100,101,125,126,...`.

**Fix.** Represent the suffix as `used` and `group`, require `used <= group`, implement containment as `(cpu - start) % group < used`, and update `expand_bounded()` to emit the first `used` CPUs from each `group` window. Replace the current grouped tests with a case such as `100-2000:2/25` that checks `100`, `101`, `125`, and `126` are included while `102` is not.

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/types.rs` line 275

> ```diff
> Some('T') => 1024u64.pow(4),
> Some('P') => 1024u64.pow(5),
> _ => return Err(ParamError::InvalidValue),
> ```

Incorrect parsing (minor): `MetricU64` rejects the Linux metric suffix `E` even though `1E` is a valid `u64` value (`1024^6`). Any parameter using this value type will reject a valid command-line size such as `mem=1E` or another `MetricU64`-backed setting.

**Fix.** Add `Some('E') => 1024u64.pow(6)` and tests for both `1E` success and an overflowing `E` value returning `ParamError::InvalidValue` through `checked_mul()`.

## Security

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/lib.rs` line 152

> ```diff
> +pub use inventory::submit;
> ...
> +            $crate::submit! {
> +                $crate::KernelParam { name: $name, setup: __kparam_setup, early: false }
> +            }
> ```

`deny-unsafe-kernel` (critical): `__define_param!` routes every kernel-parameter registration through `inventory::submit!`. That macro expands in the calling kernel crate to an `unsafe { ErasedNode::submit(...) }` constructor and relies on `inventory`'s raw global linked-list invariants, so the new parameter framework introduces hidden unsafe code into `kernel/` instead of confining it to `ostd/`.

**Fix.** Remove `inventory` from the kernel-side API. Use an explicit safe registry/list for the current parameters, or move the unsafe linker-section inventory machinery behind a small safe API in `ostd/` and have `kernel/` call only that safe wrapper.

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/lib.rs` line 481

> ```diff
> +define_unimplemented_param!(
> +    "tsc",
> +    "no_timer_check",
> +    "reboot",
> +    "pci",
> +    "debug",
> +    "panic",
> +    "nr_cpus",
> +    "selinux",
> +    "initrd"
> +);
> ```

`validate-at-boundaries` (major): `selinux` is registered as a recognized kernel parameter but `setup_unimplemented()` accepts every occurrence by only logging a warning. A boot line such as `selinux=1` requests enabling a Linux security module, yet Asterinas continues booting without SELinux enforcement; invalid values such as `selinux=garbage` are accepted the same way.

**Fix.** Do not consume security-enabling parameters as generic unimplemented placeholders. Either remove `selinux` from this list so it is not presented as recognized, or add a dedicated parser that validates `0`/`1` and fails closed for `selinux=1` until SELinux is implemented.

## Documentation

### `book/src/kernel/linux-compatibility/kernel-parameters.md` line 24

> ```diff
> 22 Select console devices for kernel messages.
> 23 This parameter may be specified multiple times.
> 24 Kernel messages are delivered to each listed console.
> ...
> 105 // TODO: Support specifying multiple TTY devices, e.g., "console=hvc0 console=tty0".
> 106 let console_name = CONSOLES
> 107     .get()
> 108     .and_then(|consoles| consoles.first().map(|s| s.as_str()))
> ```

`linux-compat-docs` (major): The `console` entry promises that repeated `console=` values receive kernel messages, but the implementation only reads `CONSOLES.first()` for `/dev/console`, and the logger sends to all registered consoles independently of `console=`. A boot line such as `console=ttyS0 console=hvc0` is documented as selecting both consoles, but only `ttyS0` is used by `SystemConsole`.

**Fix.** Update `book/src/kernel/linux-compatibility/kernel-parameters.md` to describe the current behavior: `console=` is repeatable syntactically, but Asterinas currently uses only the first value for `/dev/console` and defaults/falls back to `tty0`. Remove the claim that kernel messages are delivered to every listed console unless the code is changed to implement that behavior.

### `book/src/kernel/linux-compatibility/kernel-parameters.md` line 62

> ```diff
> 60 Valid values:
> 61 - `1`, `on`, `yes`, `true` or no value — treat the i8042 controller as present (force probing)
> 62 - `0`, `off`, `no`, `false` - treat the i8042 controller as absent (skip probing)
> ...
> 132 if !Self::is_present_acpi() {
> 144     if !Self::is_present_cmdline() {
> 149         return Err(I8042ControllerError::NotPresent);
> 156 } else {
> 157     log::info!("ACPI says i8042 controller is present");
> ```

`linux-compat-docs` (major): The `i8042.exist` docs claim that `i8042.exist=0` forces the controller absent, but `I8042_EXIST` is only consulted inside the `!is_present_acpi()` branch. If ACPI reports the controller as present, `i8042.exist=0` is ignored and probing still proceeds, so the documented skip-probing mode does not exist.

**Fix.** Either implement the documented force-absent behavior, or change the parameter documentation to say that `i8042.exist` only forces probing when ACPI reports the controller absent, and that false values merely do not force probing.

### `kernel/comps/cmdline/src/lib.rs` line 473

`linux-compat-docs` (major): This change registers `tsc`, `no_timer_check`, `reboot`, `pci`, `debug`, `panic`, `nr_cpus`, `selinux`, and `initrd` as recognized kernel parameters that are consumed and warned about, but `book/src/kernel/linux-compatibility/kernel-parameters.md` omits them. Users cannot tell from the compatibility docs that these Linux parameter names will no longer be forwarded to `init`.

**Fix.** Add a documented section for recognized-but-unimplemented kernel parameters, listing these names and saying that Asterinas consumes them and emits a warning without implementing Linux behavior; or stop registering them in this change until the compatibility page is updated.
